## China / Asia

- `9` **NAND maker YMTC’s RMB33 billion Shanghai IPO application accepted**
  YMTC’s STAR Market IPO application has been accepted (not yet approved), targeting RMB33bn (~US$4.9bn) — described as one of the market’s largest raises — earmarked for production-line upgrades and R&D. That is a concrete China NAND capacity-funding step, not a listing close. For nand-cycle and memory names (000660, SNDK, 285A), the transmission is extra YMTC bits into an already cyclical NAND market: if the raise funds a real capacity/node step, it extends oversupply/pricing risk; if it is mostly R&D/upgrade with slow conversion, the signal is weaker. Confirm or refute on final CSRC/SSE approval, disclosed wafer adds vs current YMTC output, and subsequent NAND ASP prints.
  Sources: [TechNode Feed](https://technode.com/2026/08/24/nand-maker-ymtcs-rmb33-billion-shanghai-ipo-application-accepted/)

## Custom silicon & compute

- `10` **Alchip Technologies Reports 2026 Second Quarter Financial Results**
  Alchip Q2’26 revenue was $241.7m vs $132.4m in Q1 (+83% QoQ) but -18.7% vs $297.4m a year earlier; NI $51.8m (+20.7% YoY / +14.9% QoQ) and GP $84.2m (+36.7% YoY) rose on mix. CEO Johnny Shen tied the sequential jump to 3nm accelerator shipments to a North American customer that started in May and should keep ramping in Q3; 3nm/2nm were 47% of Q2 sales, 5/7nm 40%, North America 51%, HPC/niche 51%. This is a custom-silicon (mrvl-trainium / AVGO vs NVDA) conversion datapoint: a NA 3nm XPU is in revenue, not just a design win, with advanced-node mix already dominant and TSMC CoWoS/packaging as the transmission path. Confirm if Q3 stays elevated or the customer is named (Trainium-class vs other); refute if 3nm revenue rolls off after the initial lot.
  Sources: [www.globenewswire.com](https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2026/08/26/3351381/0/en/alchip-technologies-reports-2026-second-quarter-financial-results.html)
- `9` **OpenAI reveals Jalapeño AI chip benchmark results, plans wider deployment in 2027** → [[Theses/CRWV - CoreWeave]]
  OpenAI posted first public Jalapeño (Broadcom-co-developed inference ASIC) results at Hot Chips on SemiAnalysis InferenceX vs Nvidia Blackwell: 1.5–1.9x work/watt and 1.7–3.6x lower latency on GPT-OSS 120B, DeepSeek R1 670B and Kimi K2.5 1T; 2.1–4.1x on interactive/agentic loads, and 8.6–104.3x work/watt at the prior-best inter-token operating point. Limited volume is slated late 2026, broader rollout 2027—after rival silicon will have moved—so today’s vs-Blackwell print is not a vs-Rubin comparison. Design focus is agentic prefill and on-package KV-cache locality, framed as a multi-generation platform. Touches openai-xpu and AVGO custom-ASIC revenue vs NVDA inference mix: 2026 units are too small to dent GPU demand; 2027 share risk is real only if Broadcom wafer starts and OpenAI cluster attach convert. Confirm via AVGO custom commentary and actual Jalapeño deployments; refute if Rubin/Blackwell still take the bulk of OpenAI inference.

- `9` **Huawei desafía a EEUU en Egipto con sus chips de IA y abre un nuevo frente en la guerra tecnológica**
  Huawei bid on an Egyptian government AI-DC tender with 1,408 Ascend 950 chips for a training/entertainment cluster plus ~600 more Ascend-family or 910B parts for two inference clusters, a 12-month build, partnered with Entity-listed iFlytek. The US State Department is canvassing Nvidia, AMD and Microsoft for a counter-consortium—the first cited head-to-head government AI-infra contest outside China, even at only ~2,000 accelerators. This is a huawei-ascend / export-controls beachhead in Africa/MENA, where Washington has treated Gulf AI-DC as strategic; NVDA/AMD TAM is not lost unless Cairo awards Huawei. Confirm via award or a US consortium bid; refute if Egypt delays, splits, or Washington blocks both sides.
  Sources: [businessinsider.es](https://businessinsider.es/liderazgo/huawei-desafia-eeuu-egipto-con-sus-chips-ia-abre-un-nuevo-frente-guerra-tecnologica_7029625_0.html)

## Foundry & packaging

- `8` **Nvidia’s Groq chip ramp brings Samsung foundry closer to profit - The Korea Herald**
  Nvidia said at Hot Chips that Groq 3 LPX has entered full production: a 256-LPU rack-scale inference box meant to sit beside Vera Rubin (GPUs train, LPUs generate tokens). Samsung Foundry is the sole 4nm LPU manufacturer; Huang had already said in March Nvidia is ramping “as fast as we possibly can.” KB Securities argues Samsung foundry can post its first profit since 2022 as early as Q3 on a 15% foundry price hike plus this 4nm LPU volume. Touches NVDA and rubin-ramp: Groq is complementary inference attach after the ~$20B Groq IP/talent deal, not a GPU substitute. Samsung foundry recovery is second-order (not a coverage name). Confirm on NVDA earnings (due immediately in Korea time) via LPX/Rubin attach language and on Samsung Q3 foundry OP; refute if LPX remains a niche SKU.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiowFBVV95cUxPRW9EV3NtMV85emFfLUk0TS1CWkxJeTVzTmdwSGJ4aWpwaHpNSktqXzBtVU9OLWpUR29FdDhOb1M4N0tUYXduRjFOQ0l0ZVNZVEZ0MHRzLUh1a3lVRmxsYzd5ZnZkV20wcTBQbUxSeC1fQzI3R21HYjljZ1M0RmJVMFpmaG1EYkhFc2MxU3ZScXVsM3lyS2NGZ1BGWmVUb0tWdHRr?oc=5) · [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMidkFVX3lxTE5wLXF6NEVrZ2RaRkozNmZUb0Q2OVo5VTNIU2NIMWNYVjRiVXJUZG1ZUDJHeE9VZnhIVWFuVTNrRzdsV0RYblFFX2F2OTRsVDUyak5mdUtUYXZyclN3Uk05YUxILXRJV0JZY1VMLUZhd3NBNFdQV1E?oc=5) · [koreaherald.com](https://koreaherald.com/article/10852890)
- `8` **TSMC's 310mm push threatens Samsung's PLP lead**
  Headline only: TSMC’s 310mm panel push is framed as threatening Samsung’s FOPLP lead; the body merely restates that Samsung commercialized fan-out panel-level packaging first (mobile/wearables) and is preparing to extend it into AI/HPC—no 310mm specs, timing, or customers (copos-panel / TSM).
  Sources: [digitimes.com](https://digitimes.com/news/a20260825VL220/samsung-tsmc-packaging-panel-equipment.html)
- `8` **Apple asked CXMT for a discount on DRAM — and got Samsung's price instead - Astute Group**
  CXMT refused Apple a China-iPhone DRAM discount and held prices at Samsung/SK Hynix parity; EE Times says CXMT 64GB DDR5 server modules even listed above Samsung’s ~$1,240. CXMT is ~7.7% of global DRAM (4th largest), already supplies Huawei/Xiaomi, and has a five-year $7bn ByteDance contract; Apple reportedly asked Washington for permission because CXMT is on the Pentagon Chinese Military Companies list. Capacity talk is 500k wpm by 2030 vs Micron ~375k, still DUV/multi-patterning (16/24Gb to 8000 MT/s); Micron is lobbying on ~50% undercut risk if barriers ease. This is a 000660/MU DRAM pricing-power inflection: a fourth supplier is pricing at the ceiling because AI/HBM has absorbed bits, not dumping. Confirm if DDR5 contract prices hold/rise; refute if CXMT cuts to win Apple/PC or the US blocks offtake.
  Sources: [astutegroup.com](https://astutegroup.com/news/memory-shortages/apple-asked-cxmt-for-a-discount-on-dram-and-got-samsungs-price-instead)
- `8` **China has plenty of indium but not enough InP wafers for AI data centers**
  China has >70% of primary indium output but only ~10% of finished InP substrates, a qualification/capacity gap for AI-datacenter optics (EML/SiPh) rather than a raw-material shortage (inp-capacity / china-optics; COHR, IQE, Veeco). Confirm via substrate/MOCVD utilization comments from those names; refute if Chinese InP wafer share closes the 10% gap.
  Sources: [digitimes.com](https://digitimes.com/news/a20260825VL217/inp-capacity-substrate-wafer-6-inch.html)

## General tech news

- `9` **Carry On (autonomous) Cabby (2/2) - why Uber CEO Dara Khosrowshahi is ready to slow down in order to get autonomous vehicles in the right lane**
  Uber is live with AV in 7 cities and says it is on track for 15 by year-end, with named launches (Nuro/Lucid Bay Area, Zoox Vegas, Wayve London/Tokyo, Baidu London, Pony/Verne Zagreb). It is ending the Waymo Phoenix collab while keeping Austin and Atlanta; NVIDIA-linked service is slated for LA and SF in 2027 and 28 cities globally by 2028, Rivian SF/Miami in 2028. Management cites mid-20s to low-30s trips per vehicle per day from Uber’s demand density and is collecting high-fidelity rideshare data from hundreds of sensor-equipped cars to share across partners. For UBER / uber-av this is a partner-diversification and utilization story, not Waymo displacement: Phoenix exit cuts one high-profile volume source, while the NVIDIA 2027 and 15-city YE targets are the near checkpoints. Confirm city count and trips/vehicle at YE and whether Phoenix trips are replaced; refute if city adds slip or utilization stays well below the mid-20s claim.
  Sources: [Stuart Lauchlan](https://diginomica.com/carry-autonomous-cabby-22-why-uber-ceo-dara-khosrowshahi-ready-slow-down-order-get-autonomous)
- `8` **Intel unwraps three-pronged architecture strategy to go after agentic AI**
  At Hot Chips 2026 Intel outlined a three-part agentic-AI stack: Xeon 7 ‘Diamond Rapids’ on 18A-P (up to 256 E-cores, 1.28GB LLC, 16 channels at 12,800 MT/s, 128 lanes PCIe 6.0/CXL 3.0); Crescent Island inference GPU (32 Xe3P cores, 256 XMX, up to 480GB LPDDR5X, 350W air-cooled); and Wildcat Lake Core Series 3 on 18A with Xe3 graphics and a 17 TOPS NPU. This is an architecture/process reveal, not a yield or design-win print. For INTC and intel-18A it is a positive product-roadmap datapoint that 18A-P is the Xeon 7 vehicle and that Intel is pitching low-TDP inference economics versus Nvidia, but share shift only follows if 18A ships in volume and Crescent Island lands hyperscaler/enterprise sockets. Confirm with 18A volume timing, TDP/token claims vs competing inference SKUs, and named customers; refute if 18A slips or Crescent Island stays a slide.
  Sources: [networkworld](https://www.networkworld.com/article/4214452/intel-unwraps-three-pronged-architecture-strategy-to-go-after-agentic-ai.html)

## Industry publications

- `8` **Deere expects ‘step-up’ in tariff costs in 2027**
  Deere says >$380m of tariff refunds have cut expected duty expense to $750m this year, while management flags a ‘step-up’ in tariff costs in 2027. The 2026 P&L relief is therefore refund-driven and not a lower run-rate. For DE, that implies a one-time margin cushion this year and a higher cost base next year unless refunds or mix offset it. Confirm on the next earnings print with explicit 2027 duty guidance versus the $750m 2026 figure. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [Antone Gonsalves](https://www.supplychaindive.com/news/deere-expects-step-up-in-tariff-costs-in-2027/828624/)
- `8` **Meta adds new AI subscription packages**
  Meta has launched Meta AI Core and Meta AI Premium paid tiers (Core as a mid-price step vs previously limited Premium), bundling more image/video generation with Facebook/Instagram/WhatsApp Plus. The article frames this against a stated >$600bn U.S. AI-infrastructure commitment over three years and a Reuters investigation: staff resisted a plan to replace up to 60% of roles with AI, internal code changes +220% YoY vs features reaching users +36%, and technical/security incidents +40% YoY. For META / meta-ai / meta-capex this is a monetization experiment, not yet a demand inflection: paid AI is the transmission path to offset capex, while the internal productivity/reliability data argues against treating Meta AI as a near-term efficiency or engagement engine. Confirm via disclosed AI-sub ARPU/subscribers and whether engagement or opex-per-user actually improves; refute if tiers stay niche and capex remains unmonetized.
  Sources: [Andrew Hutchinson](https://www.socialmediatoday.com/news/meta-adds-new-ai-subscription-packages/828904/)

EPS $2.22 beat $2.09; data center $89.02B beat $85.86B and hyperscale $48.71B beat $43.55B, but AI Clouds/Industrial/Enterprise missed ($40.31B vs $41.96B). ## Memory & storage

- `9` **Samsung Moves AI Compute Into DRAM : Drop - In Memory Chip Triples Inference Speed**
  Samsung showed working LPDDR5X-PIM at Hot Chips: 3x inference token throughput vs standard LPDDR5X in the same drop-in package (no PCB change), with a PIM MAC block in each of 16 banks—an advance vs earlier HBM-PIM that cut bank count. Hwang said memory is already >60% of AI accelerator BOM (52%→63% from Q1’24 to end-2025) and pitched PIM as a cheaper inference/edge path than HBM packaging. Touches hbm4/samsung-hbm and 000660 mix: if it wins AI PC/phone sockets it is an intensity/ASP risk to HBM, not a near-term HBM4 volume threat in training. Confirm with OEM design-wins; refute if it stays a conference demo.
  Sources: [techtimes.com](https://www.techtimes.com/articles/325678/20260826/samsung-moves-ai-compute-dram-drop-memory-chip-triples-inference-speed.htm) · [techtimes.com](https://techtimes.com/articles/325678/20260826/samsung-moves-ai-compute-dram-drop-memory-chip-triples-inference-speed.htm)
- `9` **Micron: HBM Requires Three Times More Wafer Area Than DDR5**
  At Hot Chips (23 Aug 2026) Micron HBM fellow Raghu Sreeramaneni said same-capacity HBM still takes ~3x the wafer area of DDR5 (HBM3E vs DDR5) and that the ratio ‘definitely would not get better’ in later gens, because HBM4 uses 256 banks vs DDR5’s 32 plus TSVs/power/I/O. Micron’s HBM4 is specified at 2,048 I/Os, >11 Gbit/s and >2.8 TB/s per stack (2.3x its HBM3E) with >20% better energy efficiency, while compute is scaling ~3x over two years vs HBM bandwidth <2x — the memory wall persists. That is an inflection for MU, 000660 and HBM pricing power (hbm4, samsung-hbm): every incremental HBM bit consumes ~3x DRAM wafer capacity vs DDR5, so HBM mix mechanically tightens commodity DRAM even if bit demand is flat. Confirm if Micron/SK/Samsung HBM4 die sizes and stack heights rise again vs HBM3E; refute if a node shrink or hybrid bonding cuts area/bit in published HBM4E specs.
  Sources: [igorslab.de](https://igorslab.de/en/micron-hbm-requires-three-times-wafer-area-ddr5-gap-widens)
- `8` **Samsung , SK Hynix stand to benefit as as Nvidia AI server costs surge**
  Server ODMs told Microsoft, Google and Oracle that Nvidia-based AI system prices will rise >15% (some ~17%) for early-2027 shipments across Vera Rubin and Grace Blackwell, with HBM/server DRAM the main driver; chip-system cost for a 1 GW campus could rise ≥$5B. Counterpoint: SK Hynix 58% of Q1 HBM revenue, Samsung 39% of Q2 DRAM (together 65%); HBM4 mix rises in 2H. TrendForce: server DRAM contracts +13–18% QoQ in Q3. Direct 000660/hbm4 and hyperscaler-guides: memory inflation is now in system ASPs, lifting HBM pricing power but pressuring 2027 ROI/MW. Confirm against the Jul 28–31 capex window and Rubin attach pricing; refute if CSPs absorb without cutting builds or accelerate custom silicon.
  Sources: [aninews.in](https://aninews.in/news/business/samsung-sk-hynix-stand-to-benefit-as-as-nvidia-ai-server-costs-surge20260825124107/)
- `8` ** [ 中 거인의 공급망 ] ①박막 증착장비  척형과기  없으면  CXMT  도 없다**
  Piotech (拓荆科技 688072.SH) said on 24 Aug it is expanding both tool shipments and capital into CXMT, and was allocated 18.24m shares (~Rmb158m, 18-month lockup) in CXMT’s 2026 STAR IPO while remaining a PECVD/ALD supplier. Thin-film deposition was 94.15% of 2024 sales (Rmb3.863bn of Rmb4.103bn); 3D-integration tools were only ~2.34% (Rmb96m) even as the firm adds W2W/D2W hybrid bonding. Installed base already includes SMIC, Hua Hong Grace, YMTC and United Semi. This is a wfe-china + CXMT expansion datapoint: China DRAM/NAND lines are deepening a domestic deposition stack and seeding hybrid bonding rather than waiting on restricted Western WFE, a second-order share/cost-curve risk to 000660/MU commodity DRAM and a hybrid-bonding TAM teaser. Confirm via CXMT wafer-start or POR disclosures naming Piotech; refute if leading CXMT layers still require AMAT/LRCX.
  Sources: [newspim.com](https://www.newspim.com/news/view/20260824000884)
- `8` **"초격차는 후공정서"…수원서 맞붙은 삼성·SK HBM 승부수[현장+] < 현장+ < 반도체 < 미래산업 < 기사본문 - 블로터**
  At ASPS 2026 in Suwon (26–28 Aug), Samsung showed physical HBM4/HBM4E stacks pairing a 1c DRAM core die with a 4nm logic base die and pitched a one-stop turnkey (memory + foundry + 2.3D/2.5D/3D, including 3nm/2nm GAA) against SK Hynix’s staged HBM3E/HBM4/HBM4E display. SK Hynix said HBM4E mass production started this June and timed iHBM (on-chip cooling) for 2027, explicitly framing share as a TSMC-led ‘global alliance’ rather than vertical integration. Glass-substrate/TGV, low-warpage RDL and hybrid-bonding tool booths were crowded, implying the HBM4 contest is already pulling Korean OSAT/materials into the same stack as TSMC CoWoS. For 000660 vs Samsung HBM (samsung-hbm, hbm4, glass-substrate, hybrid-bonding): this is a packaging-share and thermal-roadmap datapoint, not a bit-share print — confirm with HBM4E customer qualification names and whether iHBM is design-win or demo; refute if 1c-die HBM4 remains sample-only into 2027.
  Sources: [bloter.net](https://bloter.net/news/articleView.html?idxno=671833)

## Semis / hardware / datacenter

- `9` **Intel Diamond Rapids: Building Xeon Up, Out, and Through Silicon**
  Intel’s Diamond Rapids Xeon, detailed at Hot Chips, scales to 256 cores and 1.28 GB LLC via four compute building blocks (up to four 16-core chiplets on each base tile). Packaging is Foveros 3D Direct hybrid bonding for cores-to-base plus UCIe-S copper between base and fabric-hub tiles; the process is Intel 18A-P with a dual-contact PowerBoost option. Memory fabric is 16 channels (DDR5-8000 / MRDIMM-12800, ~1.6 TB/s); I/O is 128 lanes of PCIe 6.0/CXL 3.0 (~2 TB/s bidirectional) plus on-die QAT/DSA/IAA. Direct intel-18a and hybrid-bonding datapoint: 18A-P is now attached to a volume Xeon architecture, not just a process slide. Confirm with 18A-P yield/volume and Foveros capacity; refute if Diamond Rapids slips or 18A stays client-only.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMifkFVX3lxTE1kcF83d2ZLWVIyU05mX1ZDQXpGbnZ2ZWszbDd4QzNLdmdPSDNPRDRxNGVZZFVXZDJEdXNWMGNaNHlydHEtYjk1QmZEeENZdnM0TTRONWNRdDBhaV84blhwWnREbUt3R291azNtRTNrWnI5bGtzSHRBUUR2Z2hGZw?oc=5) · [Admin](https://semiwiki.com/semiconductor-manufacturers/intel/372661-intel-diamond-rapids-building-xeon-up-out-and-through-silicon/)
- `9` **Trymax Lands Multi-Year AOI Deal for InP Laser Manufacturing Equipment**
  Trymax booked a multi-year, multi-million-dollar PO from Applied Optoelectronics (AAOI) for plasma ashers and UV curing on in-house InP laser-chip production at AOI’s Sugar Land, Texas fab, handling fragile 3–6 inch InP as AOI ‘rapidly expand[s]’ capacity for intra-/inter-DC transceiver lasers; dollar size and tool counts undisclosed. Touches AAOI (captive InP vertical integration) and inp-capacity as a US laser-fab build signal; covered InP names (Veeco/COHR/IQE) are not the vendors here. Confirm with AAOI laser-chip mix/capex or follow-on InP MOCVD orders; refute if AOI remains assembly- rather than chip-constrained.
  Sources: [Stephen Las Marias](https://www.eetasia.com/trymax-lands-multi-year-aoi-deal-for-inp-laser-manufacturing-equipment/)
- `9` **NVIDIA NVLink Fusion Brings NVHBM to Next-Generation AI Infrastructure**
  NVIDIA’s NVLink Fusion blog positions custom XPUs/CPUs inside its MGX scale-up/scale-out stack, with NVHBM (custom HBM base-die, validated with leading memory vendors) claiming up to 30% more bandwidth/stack vs JEDEC HBM4e, up to 67% less PHY/support area (freeing up to 30% main-die silicon), 15% lower HBM power, and a compounded ~30% E2E lift per XPU—framed as headroom for ~15,000 extra 2 kW XPUs in a 1 GW site. First-party claims, not named design wins. Touches mrvl-fabric (Fusion vs UALink/ESUN), hbm4, and custom-silicon share (AVGO/MRVL XPUs pulled onto Nvidia’s memory/interconnect). Confirm with hyperscaler Fusion/NVHBM sockets; refute if UALink camps ship equivalent HBM PHYs or Fusion stays GPU-adjacent only.
  Sources: [nvidia-dev](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-nvlink-fusion-brings-nvhbm-to-next-generation-ai-infrastructure/)
- `8` **NVIDIA Ties $103 Billion in Commitments to OpenAI Site That Could Return $200 Billion in Revenue**
  Nvidia (NVDA) disclosed $108.5B maximum gross exposure on land/power/shell commitments (title said $103B; body $108.5B): $3.5B of default guarantees on select AI-cloud partners’ leases, with the rest credit support to secure ~4.25 GW at SB Energy’s PORTS-Pike campus in Ohio under a 20-year OpenAI lease, obligations effective as sites are ready from FY2029, plus optional phased support for another 3.8 GW (dollar amount not given). NVDA says each generation there could be ~1.5 million GPUs or $150–200B of NVDA revenue across multiple 20-year upgrade cycles; Q2 revenue +106%, net income +126%, GM 75% (+2.6 pts). This is a demand-lock / circular-financing datapoint for NVDA (OpenAI capacity, not the openai-xpu custom-chip question): credit support converts to multi-gen GPU pull-through if OpenAI occupies and refreshes the site. Confirm via FY29 occupancy and GPU shipments (and a dollar size on the extra 3.8 GW); refute if partners default or guarantees never convert to revenue.
  Sources: [Ramish Zafar](https://wccftech.com/nvidia-ties-103-billion-in-commitments-to-openai-site-that-could-return-200-billion-in-revenue/)
- `8` **Apple Moves to 2nm with M6, Quad-Die M5 Ultra for AI and Pro Workloads**
  Apple launched M6 as its first 2nm SoC (12-core CPU, 12-core GPU with Neural Accelerators, dual 16-core Neural Engine, 170 GB/s; up to 1.2x multithreaded vs M5 and 2.4x vs M1) and M5 Ultra as a quad-die UltraFusion part (up to 36-core CPU / 80-core GPU, 512 GB unified memory, 1.2 TB/s, >4.4 TB/s inter-die). M5 Ultra is pitched to run hundreds-of-billions-parameter models on-device. Implies TSMC N2 is in volume for a lead customer (tsmc-nodes), a utilization/ASP positive for TSM, not a CoWoS/HPC accelerator print. Confirm in TSMC N2 revenue-mix comments; refute if 2nm remains a thin client slice vs delayed N2 HPC.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiW0FVX3lxTE51Wnp5ZC14dlhvbUVhb0tuanhGTEpjcmlsaC1NeXJjMF95VDlpckRDNjl6bktpYzFnTTVlbFhKTlNwdTNMSmc1X21xLWhHeEdCazFiaHdiSjJkMkk?oc=5) · [Stephen Las Marias](https://www.eetasia.com/apple-moves-to-2nm-with-m6-quad-die-m5-ultra-for-ai-and-pro-workloads/)
- `8` **Cerebras Talks Going Rack-Scale with Their WSEs at Hot Chips 2026**
  At Hot Chips 2026 Cerebras detailed CS-4, its first rack-scale system putting three WSE-3 Turbo engines in one scale-up domain: WSE-3T doubles clocks, CS-4 claims 2x tokens and 10x tokens/W vs CS-3, ‘upwards of 30x’ vs a GPU, and 43,000 TB/s on-chip SRAM bandwidth; Nexus backpacks double power/cooling vs CS-3 with 50% fewer parts. Cerebras says it is already pairing WSEs with AMD’s forthcoming Instinct MI455X to mesh WSE decode with GPU racks. Touches amd-parity (MI455X as the GPU half) and rubin-ramp (explicit WSE-3T vs Rubin comparison). Implies a heterogeneous inference attach for AMD if the pairing ships; confirm at MI455X/ROCm integration or named CS-4 customers, refute if pairing stays demo-only.
  Sources: [Ryan Smith](https://www.servethehome.com/cerebras-talks-going-rack-scale-with-their-wses-at-hot-chips-2026/)
- `8` **You Probably Forget How Cheap Memory Used To Be – And Is Not So Now**
  Jim Handy (Objective Analysis) at Hot Chips 2026 framed a demand-shock vs supply-dearth across DRAM, HBM and NAND: AWS/Google/Azure/Meta dominate AI capex (Alibaba barely registers; Apple’s 2026 AI capex is below the prior five years), shared fabs mean DDR and NAND prices have ‘gone crazy,’ and the big-five memory/flash vendors are posting ‘bandit’ revenues after the late-2022/early-2023 crash, with shortages argued through at least 2030 if GenAI holds. Direct support for HBM/memory pricing power on 000660, SNDK, 285A, MU and an inverted nand-cycle (shortage, not oversupply). Confirm in hyperscaler capex guides and contract ASP prints; refute if bit growth or HBM conversion finally eases commodity DRAM/NAND.
  Sources: [nextplatform](https://www.nextplatform.com/store/2026/08/25/you-probably-forget-how-cheap-memory-used-to-be-and-is-not-so-now/5292363)
- `8` **MXFP6 and MXFP4 Mixed Precision for Accelerating Dense LLMs on AMD Instinct MI355X**
  AMD’s ROCm/vLLM blog on Instinct MI355X shows mixed W_MXFP4_A_MXFP6 recovering most of pure-MXFP4’s accuracy loss at 2–3% throughput cost and beating FP8 tokens/s: Llama-3.1-8B GSM8K 76.4% vs 62.55% MXFP4 and 80.44% FP8; Qwen3.6-27B AIME26 85.8% vs 80.0%/86.7%; offline 85.0k vs 83.3k (Llama) and 17.7k vs 16.4k (Qwen) total tokens/s vs FP8. This is MI355X software/quant, not MI455X, Helios or MLPerf. Touches AMD and amd-parity via the ROCm inference stack. Suggests near-FP8 quality at half FP8 weight bits if kernels stay in production vLLM; confirm with MLPerf or MI455X numbers, refute if recovery fails on larger MoE/reasoning models.
  Sources: [Rainww](https://www.eetasia.com/embeddedblog-mxfp6-and-mxfp4-mixed-precision-for-accelerating-dense-llms-on-amd-instinct-mi355x/)
- `8` **Maximizing AI Factory Performance per Watt with NVIDIA DSX MaxLPS**
  NVIDIA DSX MaxLPS (dynamic power software + workload power managers + 45°C-inlet Vera Rubin NVL72 liquid cooling) is projected to enable up to 40% more Rubin GPU capacity inside a 100 MW AI factory by reallocating unused rack headroom and cutting chiller/PUE; NVIDIA says ~60% of site power reaches compute under static provisioning. Touches rubin-ramp and NVDA: the 40% GPUs-per-MW figure is the mechanism that could stretch power-constrained hyperscaler capex into more Rubin tokens without new substations. Confirm if Rubin NVL72 deployments cite 45°C loops or DPS in production; refute if utility interconnect, not rack PUE, remains binding or 40% is lab-only.
  Sources: [nvidia-dev](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/maximizing-ai-factory-performance-per-watt-with-nvidia-dsx-maxlps/)
- `8` **Solving Agentic AI Fleet Challenges with NVIDIA Vera CPU**
  NVIDIA cites 163,594 agentic sessions with >97% unique trajectories and a latency-bound sequential critical path plus sporadic fan-out, arguing a single balanced Vera CPU (Olympus cores) beats a split specialized-CPU fleet; estimated SPEC CPU 2026 shows up to 1.5x per-core vs AMD Venice on compiler, static-analysis and Python agentic workloads. Touches rubin-ramp (Vera in the Rubin platform) and AMD (Venice as the foil); agentic CPU demand is orchestration around GPU factories, not a direct Intuit/x402 read. Implies Nvidia wants the agent-sandbox socket, not just the GPU. Confirm with Vera attach in NVL72 BOMs or independent SPEC; refute if hyperscalers keep AMD/Intel CPUs for tooling.
  Sources: [nvidia-dev](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/solving-agentic-ai-fleet-challenges-with-nvidia-vera-cpu/)

## ticker

- `10` **Marvell Grants Google $12.2 Billion Stake Option** → [[Theses/MRVL - Marvell Technology]]
  Marvell granted Google a warrant for up to 58.97 million shares at $206.58 (~$12.2B), tied to co-development of custom silicon around TPUs (compute, storage, networking); Marvell says ~$120B of custom-chip revenue through FY2033 if Google hits the option targets. MRVL jumped ~8% and AVGO fell >5%; Morningstar calls it incremental Google spend, not Broadcom displacement. Core custom-silicon share-shift vs NVDA and a second ASIC house for MRVL (distinct from Trainium). The $120B is target-conditional, not backlog. Confirm with disclosed Google mix in MRVL custom and whether AVGO TPU content actually shrinks; refute if the warrant expires unearned or Broadcom keeps the TPU socket.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMia0FVX3lxTE1sdjRIQVRsVk42eVI0aHFNeFRBd0JoR2x1dUhxeXlqMWc1ajVBdWRVV0JhTFlJQU4yUE52TWNOWC11TlJwS3BvbGVhUWZNb0hRSGxCVWc3NHd6aHY5allodDlvR3ZzT1hSTjJJ?oc=5) · [www.responsibleaifoundation.com](https://www.responsibleaifoundation.com/post/marvell-grants-google-12-2-billion-stake-option)
- `10` **Así es Jalapeño, el nuevo procesador con el que OpenAI quiere cambiar las reglas de la inteligencia artificial** → [[Theses/CRWV - CoreWeave]]
  At Hot Chips, OpenAI posted first silicon results for Jalapeño, its Broadcom-co-designed inference XPU: on SemiAnalysis InferenceX (GPT-OSS 120B, DeepSeek R1 670B, Kimi K2.5 1T) it showed ~1.5x peak performance/watt vs the Nvidia reference on Kimi K2.5 and up to 3.4x lower latency, running ≤550W vs 700W TDP. Richard Ho said volume is tiny in late 2026 with meaningful scale in 2027; OpenAI still keeps Nvidia, AMD, AWS, Broadcom, Cerebras, CoreWeave and Oracle. For openai-xpu and AVGO (and second-order NVDA/AMD): this is the first public efficiency claim on a custom OpenAI inference die, a 2027 share-shift risk to merchant GPU inference if the 1.5x/W holds in production clusters — training mix is explicitly not moving yet. Confirm TSMC/Broadcom tape-out volumes and whether 2027 racks displace H100/B200 inference; refute if Jalapeño stays a lab/Hot Chips demo or only fills a thin decode-specialized slice.
  Sources: [www.elespanol.com](https://www.elespanol.com/omicrono/hardware/20260826/jalapeno-nuevo-procesador-openai-quiere-cambiar-reglas-inteligencia-artificial/1003744363084_0.html)
- `10` **Nvidia's Real Story Isn't the Beat — It's What Kress Said About 2028**
  CFO Colette Kress guided FY2028 revenue growth of ~70% vs ~44% Street and said customer forecasts “point to our growth doubling next year,” still limited by supply not demand; she also said top-five hyperscaler capex should rise to $1.3T next year from $800B in 2026. GM is guided to trough in Q4 FY2027 at 71–72% on memory prices after Nvidia spent $145B in Q1 locking supply; data center is $89B, 92% of sales. Inflection for NVDA, hyperscaler-guides, and HBM names (MU, 000660, SNDK): $800B→$1.3T is the demand upgrade, the 71–72% trough is the memory-cost transmission Kress attributed to the AI buildout itself. Confirm if hyperscaler prints track that capex path; refute if memory inflation or custom silicon (AVGO/MRVL) is large enough to miss the 70%.
  Sources: [benzinga.com](https://www.benzinga.com/markets/tech/26/08/61451188/nvidias-real-story-isnt-the-beat-its-what-kress-said-about-2028)
- `9` **AI Firm AMI to Install 9k Nvidia Vera Rubin Chips** → [[Theses/RELIANCE - Reliance Industries]]
  Greenko founders’ AM Intelligence (AMI) ordered 9,000 Nvidia Vera Rubin chips and says they will be operating by Q1 2027, making AMI one of Asia’s earliest Rubin adopters; Rubin (announced January) is described as up to 10x Blackwell agent throughput and 40–80% more expensive, with supplies tight versus hyperscaler backlogs. Hyderabad is phase one of a planned 1 GW compute-as-a-service platform: 200 MW over the next 18 months at $8bn combined capex, India/Malaysia servers next year, Finland and the US in 2028, and a 5 GW global target; AMI is finalizing $1.5bn Japanese-bank GPU financing and has mandated Morgan Stanley for $1.5bn equity, with early use training then inference and a 4–5 year return target. Kolli says 50–60% of stack cost is energy, initial capacity was purchased by an unnamed (unverified) US customer, and AMI is targeting global neoclouds/enterprises rather than Indian enterprise or in-country hyperscalers. This is a named Rubin volume/timing datapoint for NVDA (rubin-ramp) and a neocloud demand signal that next-gen GPU scarcity is pulling energy-tied, non-hyperscaler capex; Q1 2027 live racks and disclosed customers would confirm, while a slipped install or a cut to the 9k order would refute tightness.
  Sources: - `9` **瑞银 ： MLCC分销商库存量进一步降至  新低 ， 单价则再度上涨** → [[Theses/6976 - Taiyo Yuden]]
  UBS Evidence Lab: as of 9 Aug, global MLCC distributor unit inventory fell another 8% in four weeks to a new historic low while inventory dollars rose 10% and the unit-price index rose 7% (YoY volume −22%, unit prices +13%, near Jan-2023 highs). SEMCO units −20% / prices +14%; Murata −8% / +6%; Yageo −1% / +7%. Murata book-to-bill 1.47 and Taiyo Yuden 1.72 in Apr–Jun, with Jul–Sep utilization guided at 95%. Direct mlcc-ai: tightening is starting in AI and distribution and UBS says it can spread to the whole market—pricing power for SEMCO/Yageo. Confirm on the next four-week inventory print and OEM hike letters; refute if this is pull-forward that restocks without further price.
  Sources: [wallstreetcn.com](https://wallstreetcn.com/articles/3780323)
- `9` **SK Hynix's Busy Week: A 25-Vote Setback, a 50% Cost Edge, and a Buyback That Keeps Delivering** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  SK Hynix (000660) production staff rejected the 2026 wage package by 25 votes (7,535–7,510, 93.81% turnout); the fight is mix, not the 6.3% base hike—labor wants more than 40% of profit-share in cash vs 60% stock. A post–19 Aug buyback is cancelling >W1tn of stock per day against a W40tn authorization and a pledge to return >50% of 2025–27 FCF; shares at W1.688m remain 15% below the 50-day MA (W1.979m). The live signal is Hot Chips: HBM4 will use Intel Foundry EMIB with a claimed up-to-50% cost edge vs competing interposers, spec’d at 2048-bit / 2 TB/s and +40% energy efficiency vs HBM3E; MR-MUF stays through HBM4E and hybrid bonding slips to HBM5 on a 775μm stack-height limit. That is an hbm4 / intel-18a (EMIB) cost-curve win for 000660 and INTC attach, and a delayed hybrid-bonding TAM for BESI/ASMI. Confirm if Nvidia-class customers qualify EMIB-HBM4; refute if EMIB is non-Nvidia-only or hybrid bonding is pulled into HBM4E.
  Sources: [www.ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/unternehmensnachrichten/sk-hynix-s-busy-week-a-25-vote-setback-a-50-percent-cost-edge-and-a/70003897)
- `9` **Neosem Sees Surge in SSD Tester Demand on Higher Capacities, QLC Shift < Supply Chain < 기사본문 - The Elec Inc.** → [[Theses/6857 - Advantest]]
  Neosem said (26 Aug, Suwon) that SSD test time has gone from ~30 minutes on 1TB to ~2.5 hours on 16TB, so holding unit output requires >2–3x testers as SKUs scale 8→16→32→64TB (roadmaps >120TB), TLC→QLC, and PCIe Gen6 PAM4 adds SI/BER/error-recovery work. Advantest exited SSD test last year, leaving Neosem a near-monopoly in PCIe Gen5 SSD testers and an early Gen6 lead; the piece cites four-hyperscaler capex up to $730bn (~+90% YoY). This is a nand-cycle / AI-storage demand signal (longer SLT/burn-in), mix-irrelevant for Advantest post-exit, and only adjacent to AEHR’s wlbi-test thesis if QLC makers add wafer-level stress. Confirm via tester orders and QLC SSD test-hour data; refute if hyperscalers stay TLC or absorb test without adding boxes.
  Sources: [thelec.net](https://thelec.net/news/articleView.html?idxno=13361)
- `9` **Huawei pitches AI chips to Egypt in test of US tech diplomacy | FMT** → [[Theses/AMD - Advanced Micro Devices]]
  Huawei answered an Egyptian tender with 1,408 top-end Ascend 950-series GPUs for an AI training cloud plus 600 more 950s or older 910Bs for two inference clusters, on a 12-month build for military/surveillance/public-sector use — potentially the first known Ascend export after more than a year of failed attempts. Washington’s State Department is assembling a counter-offer with Nvidia, AMD and Microsoft. For huawei-ascend, NVDA, AMD and export-controls: this is a live third-country channel test of whether Ascend 950 can land outside China despite BIS; a signed Cairo deal would be a share-shift signal in non-aligned AI infra, a blocked deal would show the US can still veto. Confirm chip counts in a signed contract and whether 950PR/950DT silicon actually ships; refute if Egypt takes the US bundle or the pitch dies at CFIUS/export license.
  Sources: [www.freemalaysiatoday.com](https://www.freemalaysiatoday.com/category/business/2026/08/26/huawei-courts-egypt-with-ai-chips-in-test-of-us-tech-diplomacy)
- `9` **Meta's $17 billion child-safety settlement is the biggest tech payout ever—or 3x what it paid to acquihire a 28-year-old AI superstar | Fortune** → [[Theses/META - Meta]]
  Meta agreed to pay up to $17.1bn (company says ~$18bn) to 29 states over alleged child-addictive design — the largest single tech settlement — of which ~$12.7bn (70%) is paid over 10 years regardless and ~$5.3bn is contingent on TikTok and YouTube matching time-limits/age-verification and each paying a matching sum. Versus FY2025 ($60.5bn NI, $201bn revenue) the headline is ~27% of one year’s profit / ~8% of sales; Meta had warned of a theoretical $1.4trn damages frame and states had floated ~$200bn at trial, so this is a capped, multi-year cash outflow, not a going-concern hit, while 2026 capex is still guided as high as $145bn. For META (meta-capex) and tiktok-ban: the structure tries to export Meta’s restrictions industry-wide; the $145bn AI capex path is intact unless the 10-year installment plus any matching TikTok/YouTube payments force a guide cut. Confirm the payment schedule vs FCF and whether TikTok/YouTube actually opt in (unlocking the extra $5.3bn); refute if product changes hit engagement enough to dent ads or if remaining private suits reopen the $200bn-class risk.
  Sources: [fortune.com](https://fortune.com/2026/08/26/meta-settlement-17-billion-child-safety-how-big/)
- `9` **Micron Guides to $50 Billion Revenue as AI Memory Booms - Can MU Break $1,034?** → [[Theses/MU - Micron Technology]]
  Micron posted record FYQ3 2026 revenue of $41.46B (vs $9.30B YoY and $23.86B prior quarter) with 84.6% GAAP GM, $24.67/$25.11 GAAP/non-GAAP EPS and $25.39B operating cash flow, then guided FYQ4 to $50B ±$1B (~21% sequential) at 86% GM and ~$31 non-GAAP EPS. Mix is now AI-heavy: Cloud Memory $13.77B and Core Data Center $11.52B; HBM4 is in high-volume shipments to a lead customer with qualification samples at multiple others, while HBM4E on 1-gamma is slated for calendar-2027 volume. This is an inflection-point print for memory pricing power (MU, 000660, SNDK, 285A): tightness plus HBM mix—not PC/handset recovery—is the mechanism, implying peak economics can extend if HBM4 ASP holds. Confirm if the next print keeps GM ≥86% with no customer-commitment or conventional-DRAM cracks; refute on a GM/guide cut or capacity-add commentary that breaks the $1,034.50 level the source flags as buyer-control confirmation.
  Sources: [www.tradingkey.com](https://www.tradingkey.com/analysis/stocks/us-stocks/262132895-micron-ai-memory-q4-guidance-mu-breakout-tradingkey)
- `9` **Nvidia beat on every line. Its only bear says that's the problem.**
  Nvidia beat every printed line: revenue $96.2B vs $92.38B, adj. EPS $2.22 vs $2.09, data center $89B, next-quarter guide $108B, and 75% margin vs 72.7% a year ago. The clip’s claim that “its only bear says that’s the problem” is not specified in the provided text, so treat this as confirmation of the FQ2 beat in items 1/6 rather than a new bear mechanism. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [youtube.com](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BsGXOlMjtRE)
- `9` **Jensen Huang Said Nvidia's $30 Billion OpenAI Investment Might Be the Last. Nvidia Just Guaranteed Up to $105 Billion of OpenAI's Leases.**
  An Aug. 17 document shows Nvidia residual-value guarantees capping payment at $105B on OpenAI’s 20-year leases at SB Energy’s PORTS-Pike Ohio campus (8 GW; Nvidia backs the first 4.25 GW with an option on the rest, exclusive Nvidia full stack, commissioning from 2028), plus a $1.5B investment in SB Energy. This is credit, not the equity Huang said in March “might be the last” after the $30B OpenAI / $10B Anthropic stakes; Nvidia pays only on OpenAI default or non-payment, net of remarketing, with OpenAI reimbursement and exits at year-20, early termination, or a satisfactory OpenAI credit rating. Touches NVDA, openai-xpu (locks Nvidia vs Broadcom/custom XPU) and circular financing: the $105B cap exceeds Q1 cash + marketable securities (~$50.3B + $30.2B). Confirm if an OpenAI IPO/credit rating actually sunsets the RVG; refute if more campuses need similar guarantees or if custom silicon breaks the exclusive lock.
  Sources: [fool.com](https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/08/25/jensen-huang-said-nvidia-s-usd30-billion-openai-investment-might-be-the-last-nvidia-just-guaranteed-up-to-usd105-billion-of-openai-s-leases/)
- `8` **AI boom, profit surge fuel China’s ambitious chip assembly buildout | South China Morning Post** → [[Theses/AMD - Advanced Micro Devices]]
  China’s top three OSATs (JCET, Tongfu, Huatian) committed >RMB15bn (~$2.2bn) of expansion in H1 2026 after AI-packaging profits: JCET net profit +79.4% YoY to RMB845m on record RMB19.5bn revenue, and a RMB7.8bn Shanghai Lingang advanced-packaging plant with phase-1 completion in H2 2028. Tongfu guided H1 profit up as much as 337% YoY on its AMD processor partnership, won approval for a RMB4.2bn placement and will put RMB888m into 849,600 wafers/year of memory-chip capacity. For AMD (packaging second-source) and wfe-china/cowos: this is a China advanced-packaging capacity wave timed to AI accelerators, not trailing-edge OSAT — it can absorb some CoWoS-like demand if export rules allow, or become stranded if BIS tightens. Confirm Lingang tool vendors and whether Tongfu’s extra wafers are HBM/HBM-adjacent vs commodity; refute if the RMB15bn is mostly test/assembly, not 2.5D.
  Sources: [www.scmp.com](https://www.scmp.com/tech/tech-trends/article/3365337/ai-boom-profit-surge-fuel-chinas-ambitious-chip-assembly-buildout)
- `8` **CrowdStrike adds $333 million in new annual recurring revenue, raises growth outlook** → [[Theses/CRWD - CrowdStrike Holdings]]
  CrowdStrike’s Q2 FY27 (ended 31 Jul 2026) delivered record net-new ARR of $333m (growth accelerated to 51% YoY), total ARR $5.84bn (+25%), revenue $1.47bn (+26%) and subscription $1.40bn (+27%), with Falcon Flex ARR at $2.29bn (+101% YoY). Management raised FY27 net-new ARR growth guidance by 630 bps to 34% YoY at the midpoint; Q2 FCF was a record $377m and CFO $530m, with GAAP NI swinging to +$5.3m from a $70.2m loss. For CRWD: this is a re-acceleration print (net-new ARR and Flex), not just a beat — the implied transmission is AI-security attach (Kurtz: ‘Mythos moment’) lifting module mix (51%/35%/26% on 6+/7+/8+ modules) and cash conversion. Confirm if Q3 pipeline converts at similar net-new ARR and if Flex stays >100% growth; refute if the 630 bp guide raise is mix/one-off large deals rather than new-logo breadth.
  Sources: [stocktitan.net](https://stocktitan.net/news/CRWD/crowd-strike-reports-second-quarter-fiscal-year-2027-financial-aw4xynrytp66.html)
- `8` **Intel stock steadies after $20 billion equity raise as AI revenue surges** → [[Theses/INTC - Intel]]
  Intel priced a $20bn upsized equity raise (210.5m shares at $95, ~$19.7bn net — largest since the 1971 IPO) after Q2 2026 revenue of $16.1bn (+25% YoY, +19% QoQ), a seventh straight beat, with AI-oriented lines +70% YoY and ~70% of sales; Data Center & AI was $6.3bn (+59% YoY), foundry $5.8bn (+31%) but only $293m external and a $2.1bn foundry operating loss. For INTC and intel-18a: the print shows AI mix and Gaudi demand, but the $20bn is a foundry-funding dilution event while external foundry is still rounding error — transmission is balance-sheet for 18A/EMIB, not yet a TSMC share shift.
